## Motion Authoring, Editing & Retargeting

- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.

## Character Motion Synthesis & Animation

- 2026-11-12, **Ropedia Xperience 2: A Gold Standard for Scaling Physical AI Environments and Behaviors**, Hao Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.17060](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17060)
> **Abstract:** Large foundation models such as GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5.1 have recently been shown to control robots directly from visual observations. They nevertheless remain brittle on long-horizon, contact-rich tasks in real-world environments. Further progress depends on two complementary resources: demonstrations of human behavior that supervise how tasks are performed, and interactive environments in which policies can be rolled out, evaluated, and improved in closed loop. Language models have advanced along well-characterized scaling laws; the corresponding question for physical AI is how capability scales with environments and behaviors, which first requires identifying which data to scale and how to produce and reuse such data continuously. To address this question, we introduce Ropedia Xperience 2, comprising 1,589 released metric-scale 3D scans and a collection of 4,230 egocentric recordings of 74 tasks (131 hours). We further show that each capture can be repurposed for robot learning: scenes are converted into physically interactive simulation environments, human motion is retargeted to whole-body robot trajectories, and additional robot behaviors are obtained through virtual teleoperation and automated long-horizon trajectory generation. We propose this joint capture of environments and behaviors as a gold standard for physical AI data collection, laying a data foundation for establishing scaling laws over environments and behaviors.
- 2026-11-10, **EgoHMP: Achieving Precise Human Motion Prediction in 3D Scenes via Egocentric Cues**, Xin Zhao et.al., Paper: [@2611.13654](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13654)
> **Abstract:** The complex interactive dependencies between human behavior and 3D scenes make accurately predicting human intent a major challenge for human motion prediction (HMP). While recent studies use human gaze to infer intent, gaze signals suffer from ambiguity, drift, and a reliance on eye-tracking hardware. Research in cognitive science indicates that egocentric views inherently encode potential interactions, while head motion serves as a crucial precursor in motion planning. Motivated by this, we propose EgoHMP, a novel framework that uses egocentric views and head motions as robust carriers of interaction intent. By translating this intent into an envisioning of future interactions, EgoHMP achieves precise HMP in 3D scenes. EgoHMP first employs a vision-motion alignment encoder to align the visual and motion feature spaces. Based on these aligned features, an egocentric intent predictor with a modality-aware modulation mechanism adaptively suppresses invalid interaction cues to predict an accurate interaction probability map. Furthermore, to enhance the stability and realism of the generated motions, we propose a contact-aware motion decoder. Specifically, inspired by the human cognitive process of planning trajectories prior to action execution, we first predict a global trajectory to serve as a self-prompt, effectively mitigating the optimization conflict in diffusion models. Subsequently, we introduce a multi-stage denoiser, which relieves motion artifacts by incorporating human contact modeling through a multi-stage optimization strategy. Finally, a GCN-based motion decoder is employed to synthesize physically plausible and semantically consistent motions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that EgoHMP achieves state-of-the-art performance.
- 2026-11-10, **FlashMotion: Ultra-Fast Long-Term 3D Motion Generation**, Kaixing Yang et.al., Paper: [@2611.13626](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13626)
> **Abstract:** 3D human motion generation plays a crucial role in game development, extended reality (XR), digital humans, and embodied simulation. Beyond motion generation, practical pipelines include rigging, retargeting, and rendering, making ultra-fast long-term 3D motion generation without compromising quality valuable for real-time interactive applications. Despite promising progress, high-quality long-horizon motion generation under tight inference budgets remains challenging, owing to 2 coupled bottlenecks: (1) generative strategies that require many sampling steps for high-quality 3D motion generation and (2) model architectures with substantial per-step computational and memory costs for long sequences. We present FlashMotion, a co-designed framework for high-quality long-horizon 3D motion generation under a strict inference budget, covering text-to-motion, music-to-dance, and speech-to-gesture tasks. To enhance trajectory-level efficiency, we introduce a 3D motion-space MeanFlow formulation with kinematic and dynamic consistency constraints for few-step generation, together with an Adaptive Soft masking mechanism for temporally coherent motion editing. To improve per-step efficiency, we design a lightweight non-autoregressive BiMamba architecture with condition-guided state selection and AdaIN-based condition fusion mechanism, enhancing cross-modal alignment and generalization to longer motion sequences. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FlashMotion achieves overall state-of-the-art performance in both quality and efficiency across text-to-motion, speech-to-gesture, and music-to-dance generation. The project page is available at https://sun-happy-ykx.github.io/FlashMotion-Project/
- 2026-11-09, **LHNav: Long-Horizon Humanoid Navigation via Multirate Coordination and Physics-Grounded Action Data**, Xueyang Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.12136](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12136)
> **Abstract:** We study humanoid navigation across multiple destinations under long-horizon language instructions. This task is challenging because robots must maintain progress across successive goals while continuously producing natural, stable whole-body motion through confined spaces. However, task decomposition in existing methods does not by itself ensure progress throughout execution, while waypoint-based navigation does not guarantee natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. Our key idea is to organize navigation into three complementary roles operating at different time scales: a Brain for task reasoning, a Cerebellum for local motion generation, and a Brainstem for whole-body execution. We realize this idea in LHNav, an asynchronous system that uses progress and arrival feedback to coordinate navigation goals, VLA motion-token prediction, and whole-body control. To train the Cerebellum for natural, route-faithful motion, we develop a physics-grounded data engine that improves physical tracking of motion references along collision-aware routes and pairs the resulting demonstrations with rendered visual observations. Together, these designs allow task planning to proceed alongside continuous movement and extend single-goal navigation to successive destinations with natural, route-faithful whole-body motion. In simulation, our navigation VLA outperforms OmniNav and DualVLN in single-goal success and motion smoothness, with fewer collisions and falls. Using a VLA trained only on single-goal demonstrations, LHNav achieves a 75.56% success rate on long-horizon navigation tasks. Deployment on the Unitree G1 further demonstrates single-goal navigation and zero-shot long-horizon task execution in real-world environments, including layouts with repositioned objects. Project website: https://robbyant-research.github.io/LHNav/.
- 2026-11-08, **Kimodo-Policy: Learning Kinematic Action Models for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation**, Yunheng Wang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11554](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11554)
> **Abstract:** Humanoid loco-manipulation requires continuous coordination of high-dimensional whole-body motion to realize task objectives. While vision-language-action models and world action models transfer semantic and visual-dynamics priors to robot control, these priors do not necessarily capture the structure of robot motion. We introduce Kinematic Action Models (KAMs), a paradigm that treats whole-body motion as a direct pretraining target and uses a pretrained motion generator as the primary action-generation backbone. KAMs ground learned temporal and cross-body coordination priors through task conditioning, visual observations, and execution feedback. We present Kimodo-Policy as an initial instantiation, combining a frozen Kimodo text-to-motion backbone with lightweight ControlNet-based visual conditioning. A root-body-hand hierarchy coordinates locomotion and manipulation, while motion-history conditioning and repeated action-chunk prediction bridge motion synthesis and closed-loop control. Following large-scale loco-manipulation pretraining, the policy achieves the highest overall success among compared policies on multi-task HumanoidArena (72.14%), competitive performance on SIMPLE, and 60% mean success across eight real-world tasks, with 678M parameters (92M trainable). Ablations support the contribution of the pretrained motion backbone. Together, these results provide initial evidence for motion generation as a complementary foundation for humanoid action learning.
- 2026-11-08, **InfCoiL: Coordinated Planner-Controller Learning for Closed-Loop Physics-Based Human-Object Interaction**, Yude Zou et.al., Paper: [@2611.11517](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11517)
> **Abstract:** Text- and goal-conditioned human-object interaction (HOI) provides an intuitive interface for specifying high-level intents in physics-based character animation. Mapping such intents to physically plausible, contact-rich behaviors naturally calls for a generative planner and a physics-based controller, yet existing planner-controller systems either execute plans open-loop or close the loop while keeping the planner fixed, so execution feedback never reshapes what is planned. We present InfCoiL, a closed-loop framework for physics-based HOI from coordinated planner-controller learning. InfCoiL couples a morphology-aware flow-matching planner with a unified multi-morphology controller through a shared morphology interface in an autoregressive plan-and-execute loop, and alternately fine-tunes both components with reinforcement learning from the same physical rollouts. To optimize the flow-matching planner online, we introduce Semantic Flow Policy Optimization (SFPO), which stochasticizes selected denoising transitions with channel-wise semantic covariance over heterogeneous HOI features and assigns group-relative, sequence-level rewards from complete closed-loop executions. On held-out OMOMO sequences, InfCoiL outperforms open-loop hierarchical baselines in goal-reaching task success and physical tracking accuracy. Project page: https://infcoil.github.io/
- 2026-11-08, **Does Pose Encoding Matter? Analyzing Geometric Representations for Discrete Motion Vocabulary Learning**, Amir Kimiyaie et.al., Paper: [@2611.11093](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11093)
> **Abstract:** Compressing continuous human motion into a finite vocabulary of discrete tokens is now the first stage of many motion understanding and generation pipelines. Vector quantization partitions a latent space into Voronoi cells around a finite codebook, but the geometry of that latent space is inherited from the input pose encoding, and it is unclear which encoding admits an efficient discretization. We study four encodings that place skeletal motion in different geometric spaces: Gaussian joint heatmaps on the pixel lattice, which we show to be a morphological dilation of the joint point set by a quadratic structuring function; root-relative joint positions in Euclidean space; local joint rotations on $\SO$ through a continuous 6D embedding; and a redundant full kinematic encoding. For each, we train VQ-VAE tokenizers whose codebooks lie on the unit hypersphere, varying codebook size, temporal stride, and code dimension. Beyond reconstruction, we measure codebook utilization and the effective vocabulary size, i.e., the number of equiprobable spherical Voronoi cells with the same entropy as the observed token distribution. Rotation and position encodings keep converting additional codewords into effective vocabulary, whereas the full kinematic encoding saturates at a few hundred effective cells and heatmaps at fewer than sixty. The smallest code dimension gives the highest utilization for three of the four encodings, and the effect of temporal stride depends on the encoding. Encodings of the same pose family can thus yield effective vocabularies differing by an order of magnitude, a difference that reconstruction loss alone does not reveal, which points to input geometry as a first-order design choice in motion tokenization.
- 2026-11-07, **MotionGraphicsBench: Evaluating Motion Graphics Generation with Programmatic Verification**, Jiaju Ma et.al., Paper: [@2611.10592](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10592)
> **Abstract:** Large language models can generate motion graphics from static images and natural language requests, but it remains difficult to evaluate how well models can follow requests that combine spatial, appearance, and temporal requirements. We introduce MotionGraphicsBench, an expert-curated benchmark of 100 instances for animating an input static SVG with a motion edit prompt. By supplying all required visual elements, our editing task reflects a designer-like workflow and isolates motion generation from graphic generation. Derived from real-world Lottie motion designs, our benchmark contains diverse combinations of spatio-temporal animation behaviors. Each benchmark instance pairs its model input with a deterministic, human-readable verification program that accepts different valid implementations and reports which prompt requirements pass or fail, rather than comparing against a reference animation or using a VLM judge. We construct these programs with a multi-agent pipeline using human-labeled animations and synthesized test cases. On a balanced test set of 180 animations, our programs achieve 93.3% raw agreement with human judgments, compared with 83.3% for a GPT-6 Astra VLM baseline, and outperform the baseline in both approval and rejection recall. Across 47 configurations from 32 LLMs and 12 providers, pass@1 ranges from 6.0% to 67.7%. Across 15 models evaluated at their highest available reasoning effort with 32 attempts per instance, additional attempts improve every model, but pass@32 still ranges from 38.0% to 95.0%. Our analysis of verification programs reveals that generated animations often fail to match reference poses, follow requested motion trajectories, or coordinate related events. We release our benchmark at https://huggingface.co/datasets/figma-ai/motion-graphics-bench.
- 2026-11-06, **Improving the Diffusability of Motion Tokenizer**, Guanhe Huang et.al., Paper: [@2611.10160](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10160)
> **Abstract:** Latent diffusion models paired with a variational auto-encoder (VAE) tokenizer have shown promising performance for efficient text-driven human motion generation. However, motion latent diffusion suffers from a generation-reconstruction trade-off: simply increasing tokenizer capacity enhances reconstruction but fails to proportionally improve text-conditioned generation quality. We assume this trade-off stems from a spectral mismatch inside the VAE tokenizer: the encoder yields a latent space biased toward high frequencies, and the decoder introduces temporal artifacts. In this paper, we propose the Diffusable Motion Tokenizer (DiMoT) to improve the diffusability of the tokenizer, rather than scaling up the diffusion backbone. To improve text-conditioned optimization, Critic Spectral Adaptation (CSA) leverages a pretrained diffusion model as the critic to suppress high-frequency latent energy. To preserve motion dynamics, the Alpha-Flow Motion Decoder (AMD) performs generative decoding via average-velocity prediction, enabling efficient one-step decoding. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiMoT achieves state-of-the-art performance across various challenging text-to-motion benchmarks, yielding superior motion fidelity and text-motion alignment. Project page: https://githubhgh.github.io/dimot/.
- 2026-11-05, **EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling**, Yanlin Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.08477](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08477)
> **Abstract:** Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation for egocentric manipulation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning six action-video tasks. With only narrow-domain data, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.
- 2026-11-05, **REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals**, P. de Clermont Gallerande et.al., Paper: [@2611.08301](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08301)
> **Abstract:** Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.
- 2026-11-05, **IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing**, Zhoutong Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.07437](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07437)
> **Abstract:** We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.
- 2026-11-04, **Semantic Residual Vector Quantization for Sign Language Generation**, Chang Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06781](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06781)
> **Abstract:** Sign language generation (SLG) maps written sentences into signing motion while preserving both lexical content and fine-grained articulation. This requires a model to decide what signs should be expressed and to realize them through precise handshape, trajectory, facial expression and body coordination. Existing pose-based systems and reconstruction-only motion tokenizers often entangle these two decisions, leaving the generator without an explicit text-aligned intermediate target for what to sign before refining how to sign it. To address this issue, we introduce Semantic Residual Vector Quantization (SRVQ), a framework for SLG that frames generation as a two-level process moving from semantics to kinematics. The newly introduced SRV Quantizer learns a hierarchy in which the first residual level encodes sign content and subsequent levels refine pose, handshape, trajectory, and body coordination, all within a single quantizer and without separate semantic representations or external planning stages. Through word-aware conditioning for hierarchical code generation, a masked semantic generator predicts semantic codes and a kinematic generator completes the residual codes for full SMPL-X motion synthesis. We evaluate using retrieval and FID in a shared text-motion embedding space, alongside standard geometric metrics. On CSL-Daily and Phoenix-2014T, SRVQ achieves R@1 of 82.3 and 70.3, outperforming the previous SOTA by 30 and 46 points, respectively.
- 2026-11-04, **Human Motion Prediction via Continual Prior Compensation**, Jianwei Tang et.al., Paper: [@2611.06637](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06637)
> **Abstract:** Human Motion Prediction (HMP) aims to predict future human poses at different moments according to observed past motion sequences. Previous approaches mainly treated the prediction of different temporal moments as a single prediction task and learned the predictions of varied moments simultaneously, which would encounter a main limitation: the learning of short-term predictions (referring to ``near-future'' prediction) could be hindered by the predictions of long-term (referring to ``far-future'' prediction) motions. In this paper, we develop a novel temporal continual learning framework called Continual Prior Compensation (CPC) to progressively train HMP models, in which we divide the prediction task of motions corresponding to varied temporal moments into several subtasks and train the model in a multi-stage manner. To mitigate the prior information forgetting in the progressive training, we further introduce a learnable random variable Prior Compensation Factor (PCF) to explicitly measure the prior knowledge loss. We theoretically show that the PCF can be efficiently learned together with the model parameters by minimizing a reasonable upper bound of the objective function. The proposed CPC is further enhanced to estimate the prior information loss for each subtask and a new framework called Continual Prior Compensation++ (CPC++) with Fine-Grained Prior Compensation Factor (FGPCF) is finally developed. Our CPC and CPC++ frameworks are quite flexible and can be easily integrated with different HMP backbone models and adapted to various datasets and applications. Extensive experiments on three HMP benchmark datasets using multiple SOTA HMP backbones (PGBIG, siMLPe, MotionMixer, and LTD) demonstrate the effectiveness and flexibility of our frameworks.
- 2026-11-03, **Möbius: A Unified Motion-Language Model Bridging Understanding and Generation**, Jiazhi Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.05279](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05279)
> **Abstract:** We aim to build a truly unified motion model that can understand, generate, and edit human motion within a single framework. Existing motion UMMs remain only partially unified: motion understanding is handled by a language model, while generation is often delegated to a separate diffusion module operating over learned motion latents. We introduce Möbius, which brings the entire diffusion process into the same multimodal representation space used for motion understanding. Clean motion, noisy motion, and text jointly participate in shared attention, while role-specific parameters process understanding and generation streams. Importantly, Möbius operates directly on continuous frame-level raw motion, requiring neither discrete tokenization nor a pretrained motion VAE. This simple formulation naturally supports motion captioning, text-to-motion generation, temporal completion, and instruction-guided editing within a single model. On HumanML3D, Möbius achieves state-of-the-art performance among unified motion models. Scaling to 1.7B parameters and 1,700 hours of motion data further demonstrates that our framework is both effective and scalable.
- 2026-11-01, **MoPort: A View-Aware 2D Interface for Motion Language Models**, Lixing Tan et.al., Paper: [@2611.01541](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01541)
> **Abstract:** Motion language models (MoLMs) encode 3D human motion as discrete tokens for language-based understanding. Deploying them on monocular video raises two problems. Pose detection yields 2D joint sequences, leaving a dimensional gap from the 3D motion used in training. Moreover, the 2D observation changes with the camera viewpoint, while the depth lost in projection makes the 2D pose ambiguous across viewpoints. We propose MoPort, a view-aware 2D interface that enables MoLMs to take 2D observations as input. We introduce Paired View Alignment (PVA), which gives two views of the same motion a shared 3D teacher target and directly constrains the consistency of their latents, so that 2D observations from different viewpoints are mapped to a consistent semantic representation. Camera-Modulated Encoding (CME) estimates the camera extrinsics from the 2D sequence and uses them to modulate encoding, providing the 2D latents with geometric information about the viewpoint. MoPort achieves R@1 of 0.5674 on HumanML3D and 0.3286 on KIT-ML, outperforming all evaluated baselines. On HumanML3D, its R@1 decreases only from 0.5763 on in-distribution viewpoints to 0.5585 on out-of-distribution viewpoints. We validate portability on three downstream MoLMs. Furthermore, on the real-world RGB video dataset Fit3D, MoPort achieves 80.36\% accuracy. Code will be released upon acceptance.
- 2026-10-30, **Movens-Zero: Scaling Humanoid Motion Foundation Models with Large-Scale Human Videos**, Wendong Bu et.al., Paper: [@2610.41877](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41877)
> **Abstract:** Scaling humanoid motion foundation models is constrained by the scarcity of diverse, robot-executable data and by the difficulty of jointly learning from heterogeneous multimodal inputs while supporting real-time closed-loop control. We present Movens-5M, constructed from raw Internet videos through clip generation, quality filtering, semantic annotation, motion recovery, and physical refinement with humanoid retargeting and execution verification. It contains 15 million human-motion sequences and 5 million physically executable humanoid-motion sequences spanning over 10,000 hours, aligned with text, RGB video, speech, and music. Building on this data, we introduce Movens-Zero, a scalable understanding-generation-execution architecture that supports joint learning across modalities and real-time closed-loop execution. Task-mixed flow-matching training jointly optimizes a single action expert across seven generation tasks, enabling direct motion generation from text, speech, music, and video. Multimodal Real-Time Chunking connects overlapping chunks under continuously updated instructions and robot states for continuous closed-loop execution. We further introduce Movens-Intent, a four-track benchmark with 8,192 expert-verified condition-motion pairs across text, speech, music, and video. Experiments show consistent gains across all four modalities when scaling execution-verified data from 1% to 100% and action-expert capacity from 64M to 911M parameters, while demonstrating that data quality and cross-modal supervision improve generation and execution.

## Vision-Language & Action Models (VLA/VLM)

- 2026-11-12, **Validation-Guided Prompt Selection for Differential Morphing Attack Detection**, Raghavendra Ramachandra et.al., Paper: [@2611.17176](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17176)
> **Abstract:** Differential morphing attack detection (D-MAD) compares a trusted live capture with a document image that may contain a facial morph. We investigate whether labelled validation data can improve a frozen-parameter vision-language evaluator through prompt selection rather than model fine-tuning. Version~1 generates 50 candidate prompts and selects the top-ranked instruction using a security-oriented loss that prioritises missed morph attacks (MACER) while accounting for bona fide classification errors (BSCER), response failures, parse failures, and decision inconsistency. Version~2 recursively revises prompts using aggregate validation feedback. On a subject-disjoint test set of 160 pairs spanning LMA, MIPGAN-2, and PIPE, the selected Version~1 prompt achieves a (19.17\%) morph attack classification error rate (MACER), with (0.00\%) observed bona fide sample classification error rate (BSCER), compared with (70.00\%) MACER for a controlled manual prompt. The paired (50.83) percentage-point difference has a (95\%) subject-bootstrap interval of ([43.33,58.33]). Recursive revision does not surpass the best Generation~0 validation loss; the reported revised descendant obtains (31.67\%) test MACER. All 23 attacks missed by Version~1 are landmark-based (LMA) morphs. Overall, the results show that prompt formulation can substantially influence frozen-parameter VLM-based D-MAD performance, while also highlighting the challenge of recursive prompt refinement under a compact 10-subject validation setting.
- 2026-11-12, **Inner Product Regularization for Imitation Learning**, Benjamin A. Christie et.al., Paper: [@2611.17159](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17159)
> **Abstract:** Robots that learn from demonstrations should extrapolate their policy despite state perturbations such as sensor noise, scene variation, and compounding errors. One approach to this robustness is regularization. Prior regularizers consider each state on its own and limit how sensitive the policy is to any perturbation at that state. By contrast, we explore how the policy's output shifts when given the same perturbation at different states. Our insight is that, locally, the robot should respond to state perturbations in a consistent manner: e.g., if changing an irrelevant object position does not impact the policy at one state, that same change should not shift the policy output at a nearby state. Applying this insight, we develop inner product regularization (InneR). We show that the inner product on action space induces an inner product on state space through the policy Jacobian (the Fisher information for stochastic policies), and that the null space of this inner product governs which state perturbations have a first-order effect on the policy. By clustering states whose inner products are aligned, averaging the inner products within each cluster, and minimizing the smallest eigenvalues of that average, InneR shapes the learned policy so that nearby states share a common null space. InneR enforces consistent responses to perturbations without limiting policy expressiveness, and can be appended to existing imitation learning pipelines for deterministic or stochastic policies. In simulated experiments, InneR makes learned policies more robust to irrelevant state dimensions and shifts between training and test distributions, whereas norm penalties often perform worse than behavior cloning. On a real robot arm performing three manipulation tasks, InneR raises the average success rate of a diffusion policy from 18% to 57% without additional data.
- 2026-11-12, **SALA: Semantically Aligned Latent Actions Enable Dual-Axis Scaling of Embodied Foundation Models**, Jiachun Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.17110](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17110)
> **Abstract:** Embodied foundation models hinge on diverse robot interaction data, which are costly to collect. Egocentric human videos offer a complementary source of experience, and latent action models (LAMs) provide a popular means of leveraging these videos for policy supervision. Compared with world-action models (WAMs), LAMs can offer greater training efficiency and potentially lower inference costs by avoiding explicit pixel prediction during policy learning and execution. Specifically, we translate these similarities into soft neighborhood structures that guide human latent actions toward semantically related robot latents, while robot action reconstruction grounds the latent space in control. The resulting shared latent action space provides a unified supervision signal for training an embodied foundation model on both human and robot videos. We trace the failure to a temporal shortcut: because demonstrations proceed at a steady pace, elapsed time predicts progress. Indeed, rescaling the time stamps alone makes the policy stop early when the clock runs fast and repeat when it runs slow. Surprisingly, removing the time encoding does not cure the failure, because the frames of a null event still occupy slots of the time-sampled memory. Across RoboMME's released designs, vulnerability follows how memory is indexed. Of the fixes the diagnosis suggests, training with injected pauses does not remove the failure and impairs counting, whereas indexing memory by events recovers half or more of the loss without retraining. A memory meant to remember what happened should count events rather than clock ticks.
- 2026-11-10, **C$^2$-Routing: Complementary Cropping and Compression for Efficient Vision-Language Inference**, Guorui Lu et.al., Paper: [@2611.14469](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14469)
> **Abstract:** Visual Question Answering (VQA) imposes substantial computational overhead on vision-language models (VLMs), motivating model-agnostic input reduction methods that can be readily applied across diverse VLMs, including in black-box settings. Simply enforcing a visual-token cap reduces computation, but it can discard information needed to answer the question. A single reduction strategy also struggles to accommodate different scenarios. Our key observation is that cropping and compression are complementary: cropping highlights small targets and local details, whereas whole-image compression better preserves context for large targets or distributed evidence. Based on this observation, we propose C$^2$-Routing, which adaptively selects cropping or compression according to the input image and question. Since both strategies may still discard key information, we further design a safety gate that uses the predicted joint correctness distribution under original and reduced inputs to guide fallback and protect accuracy. To accommodate consumer GPU resource constraints, all training and evaluation experiments run on RTX 3090 GPUs. To measure gains beyond token capping, we use full-image baselines with the same cap of 1,024 visual tokens. We conduct extensive evaluation on three VLMs and 18 datasets spanning local details, global scenes, scene text, and documents. ## World Models & Generative Video

- 2026-11-12, **Back to Pixels: Lifting Latent Video Diffusion into Pixel Space**, Yanke Song et.al., Paper: [@2611.17336](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17336)
> **Abstract:** Frontier video generation models work in a VAE latent space that compresses video in both space and time. This compression made training tractable, but now limits high-resolution, controllable, and interactive generation -- doubling resolution quadruples token count, conditions apply to groups of frames rather than individual ones, and each response must wait for the rest of its multi-frame block. We present PixVid, a simple yet effective method that lifts a pretrained latent video model to directly generate every frame in pixel space. PixVid keeps the diffusion transformer and replaces the VAE decoder with a convolutional head that renders its outputs into pixels. We find that predicting clean video rather than velocity, together with a larger noise shift, is key to quality. Finetuned from Wan2.1-14B, PixVid is competitive with the latent baseline within $\sim 1.3$M training clips. Working framewise in pixels, it generates at $2\times$ higher resolution with the same per-frame token count, keeps depth-conditioned video editing aligned under fast motion, and responds to each action at the next frame. Project page: https://chenshuo20.github.io/PixVid/
- 2026-11-12, **K2V: Generative Keyframing For Physically Plausible Video Generation**, Felix O'Mahony et.al., Paper: [@2611.17324](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17324)
> **Abstract:** Video diffusion models are able to generate samples with high-quality appearance and textural qualities. However, content produced by these methods is known to exhibit significant physical errors. > **Abstract:** Spatial intelligence requires connecting visual observations to the geometry, objects, and persistent state of an environment. - 2026-11-04, **NextGAN: Towards Simple and Scalable GAN Training for Image and Video Generation**, Xinhao Lou et.al., Paper: [@2611.06270](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06270)
> **Abstract:** Adversarial training enables one- and few-step image and video generation, but conventional adversarial losses supervise critic values without directly specifying the gradients used to update the generator. For a fixed generator, this regression is equivalent to an adversarial objective with a gradient-energy penalty, while the generator retains the standard potential-based GAN update. The same objective adapts pre-trained image and video generators with up to 14B parameters, without auxiliary losses, online teacher scores, or trajectories. On ImageNet $256 \times 256$, NEXTGAN achieves one-step FIDs of 2.87 with pixel-space JiT-B and 1.54 with latent-space DiT-XL/1; its adversarial form further reaches 1.36. For text-to-video generation, a single trained Wan2.1-1.3B model supports one-, two-, and four-step sampling, achieving the highest VBench total scores among the compared 1.3B methods at one and two steps. At two steps, NEXTGAN scores 83.13, exceeding rCM and AnyFlow by 1.25 and 0.47 points, respectively. Code is available at https://github.com/LINs-lab/NextGAN.git.
- 2026-11-04, **ReactWorld: Structuring Interaction Responses for Embodied Video Generation**, Hanwei Liu et.al., Paper: [@2611.06120](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06120)
> **Abstract:** Embodied video world models predict scene changes caused by robot actions, yet generated object responses may remain inconsistent with task intent and contact dynamics. Critical responses in embodied scenes often concentrate in local regions and specific time intervals. Inspired by chain-of-thought reasoning, we introduce ReactWorld, an embodied video generation model guided by structured interaction anticipation. In the generation backbone's feature space, an interaction response field predictor anticipates how the interaction will unfold, explicitly describing participant roles, future motion response intensity, and local motion changes. A response feedback adapter forms increments to object features from the difference between the object branch's outputs with predicted and zeroed local dynamics inputs. It also converts predicted displacements into positional biases for temporal attention. These two mechanisms guide object feature updates and information retrieval across frames, respectively. Through interaction anticipation and generative feedback, ReactWorld achieves an average score of 77.51 on RBench, outperforming PhysisForcing by 3.29 points with improvements in all nine categories. On EZS-Bench, it achieves a Domain score of 86.98 and an average score of 81.87.
- 2026-11-04, **DecoupAttn: Decoupled Sparse Attention for Training-Free Acceleration of Video Diffusion**, Yanjin Li et.al., Paper: [@2611.05917](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05917)
> **Abstract:** Sparse attention accelerates few-step video diffusion transformers by computing a few key blocks exactly and compensating for the rest. However, existing sparse attention still faces key challenges, including identification overhead from finding critical blocks at every denoising step, suboptimal budget allocation that sets sparsity budgets uniformly or by local metrics, and cross-step error accumulation as reused masks or clusters go stale. Observing that the errors left on skipped blocks cancel each other once every key is compensated, we propose DecoupAttn, a new paradigm of decoupled sparse attention: semantic clusters compensate every key, and exact attention corrects their error on a few blocks fixed once offline by position. To support this paradigm, we introduce Distortion Budget, a rate-distortion objective for budget allocation that places exact computation across timesteps, layers, and heads where it removes the most distortion that compensation leaves in the final latent. Built on this budget, we further propose Reassign Cache, in which each step reassigns the drifting keys to cached centroids and refreshes compensation while the budgeted blocks stay fixed. Our code will be released after submission.
- 2026-11-04, **Chronos: Sequential Identity-Preserving Video Generation by Temporal Anchoring**, Mengqi Huang et.al., Paper: [@2611.05893](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05893)
> **Abstract:** Identity-preserving video generation aims to synthesize high-quality and visually realistic videos from textual and visual conditions while faithfully maintaining the subject's identity across diverse motions. Prior methods have extensively introduced reference-guided control signals and identity-aware conditioning to improve facial alignment from reference images. However, improving static identity alignment alone creates a fundamental tension between identity fidelity and temporal controllability, which frequently causes identity drift or motion suppression under large pose variations and multi-segment sequential actions. To address this, we propose Chronos, a framework that unifies single-segment facial identity preservation and multi-segment sequential action generation under a common temporal formulation. ## Computational Design, Mechanism & Fabrication

- 2026-11-10, **Optimization-Based Path Planning for Industrial Manipulators: Experimental Validation in Additive Manufacturing**, Shiva Ganesh Murali et.al., Paper: [@2611.13983](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13983)
> **Abstract:** Robotic additive manufacturing requires joint paths that reconcile robot feasibility with process-specific geometry. We develop an offline optimization-based path planner that combines joint-path regularity and self-collision penalties with process costs and constraints, so that a new process enters through its own reference, residuals, and tolerances. Full-path and boundary-coupled chunked optimization handle references of different lengths. Iterative Weight Tuning (IWT) determines the cost weights from the gaps between Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and their targets. In carbon-fiber winding and robotic 3D printing, the planned paths meet the geometric tolerances and all KPI targets, and both paths are executed on a UR10e, without extrusion for printing, with joint-level tracking recorded during execution.

> **Abstract:** Integrating three-dimensional cellular automata (3D CA) into extended reality (XR) requires coordinated simulation, volumetric data management, rendering, and interaction under variable hardware constraints. The proposed five-layer architecture integrates voxel and rule management, parallel computation, interoperability middleware, visualization, and XR interaction. Its key principles include hardware-aware execution, asynchronous simulation-rendering, adaptive fidelity, and cross-engine interoperability. An analytical evaluation framework is defined to assess performance, latency, scalability, memory efficiency, simulation correctness, and interaction quality. The proposed framework provides a foundation for developing portable and interactive 3D CA systems across desktop and mobile XR platforms.
- 2026-11-09, **Same Physics, Different Gradients: A State-of-the-Art Report on Differentiable Physics Simulation**, Lei Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.12162](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12162)
> **Abstract:** Differentiable simulation propagates derivatives through a physics simulator, turning animation, system identification, control, and computational design into gradient-based optimization. Engines differ in the physics they model and, less visibly, in which mathematical object they differentiate. We separate two questions the literature conflates. The referent is which object the returned derivative belongs to: the finite computation that executed, the converged equation it approximates, or the continuous dynamics. The realization describes the organization of the backward pass: automatically generated differentiation, an equation-level adjoint solve, or a custom adjoint. The referent defines the target against which exactness is assessed; the implementation determines how accurately that target is approximated. Cost depends on the realization together with how the states needed by the backward pass are retained or recovered. Crossing the axes places every method and engine we classify in a cell, separates claims the literature merges, and shows that the apparent trade-off between exactness and memory follows from how methods have been built rather than from a constraint. A second, orthogonal taxonomy covers contact, where the map is non-smooth. We then ask whether a correct gradient helps. Four mechanisms by which useful gradients are lost are cataloged and measured on one testbed, where the advantage of the exact derivative over the equation-level adjoint depends on where the fitting target comes from as much as on the solver budget, and we review the evidence that zeroth-order methods can outperform simulator gradients in the regimes where those mechanisms act. The model classes, systems techniques and applications are surveyed through this lens, and we close with a reporting checklist, a released diagnostic that computes it, and a research agenda.
- 2026-11-09, **Design of a hybrid tendon-linkage finger with coupled and adaptive motion**, Hyojae Kang et.al., Paper: [@2611.11885](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11885)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous robotic fingers require coordinated joint motion and adaptability to object contact, while independently actuating multiple joints increases the complexity of the transmission mechanism. > **Abstract:** Recognizing geometric texture patterns on 3D models is critical for applications such as heritage protection, model editing, and 3D printing. - 2026-11-02, **Learning Purposeful Co-designs of Bio-inspired Quadrupedal Robots**, Fabio Boekel et.al., Paper: [@2611.02462](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02462)
> **Abstract:** Treating morphology and control as separate design problems has constrained the efficiency and adaptability of quadrupedal robots, while current co-design approaches are often constrained by restricted design spaces. - 2026-10-30, **Toolpath Overhang Optimization for the Large-Format 3D Printing of Bespoke Shapes**, Yefan Zhi et.al., Paper: [@2611.00371](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00371)
> **Abstract:** This paper presents a closed curve trimming algorithm for overhang optimization in large-format support-free 3D printing of bespoke manifold shapes. Because of the material properties and surface quality requirements, such shapes are typically printed without support structures and have to be designed as support-free. Most current methods of overhang optimization are limited to certain types of geometry and do not apply to bespoke shapes. Our method directly optimizes sliced toolpath curves with minimal geometric modification. Given an overhang limit, it ensures that all resulting toolpath curves are strictly within the overhang limit while preserving their orientation and preventing intersections. The algorithm is based on curve offsetting and trimming, and is applicable to closed toolpath curves that represent the boundaries of a solid volume.

## Sketch-based & Creative HCI Tools

- 2026-11-09, **Creative Intent as an Evolving Shared State: Supporting Cross-Stage Human-AI Video Co-Creation**, Yuhang Jin et.al., Paper: [@2611.12605](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12605)
> **Abstract:** As generative AI becomes increasingly involved in video creation, creators face challenges in expanding, translating, and negotiating creative intent across stages. We therefore view creative intent as an evolving shared state throughout human-AI co-creation. A multi-stage design was employed: semi-structured interviews with 12 creators informed 3 design goals, followed by expert co-design and the development of VideoMaker. VideoMaker organizes creation into a six-stage workflow that progressively translates creative intent. At key stages, creators negotiate with AI through the Seed Panel, explicitly confirming, revising, and updating intent. A shared AI Agent, supported by the Multi-style Script Library and Cinematic Language Knowledge Base, enables differentiated expansion of initial intent and faithful translation. A within-subjects study with 30 participants showed that VideoMaker effectively supported the expansion and cross-stage translation of creative intent, with stronger intent preservation, while negotiated boundaries between creator-confirmed intent and AI initiative remained difficult to sustain.
- 2026-11-09, **Co-Creating Neurorehabilitation Technology Alongside Lived Experience Experts: A Participatory Design Workshop**, Irene L. Y. Beck et.al., Paper: [@2611.12202](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12202)
> **Abstract:** Involving patients' lived experience (LE) early in neurorehabilitation technology design can improve the relevance and effectiveness of solutions, yet LE experts are often involved only when finished prototypes are tested. We present and evaluate a participatory design (PD) workshop for early LE involvement, examine how LE contributions influence design processes and outcomes, and provide recommendations for similar approaches. We conducted a PD workshop at the 6th International Conference on NeuroRehabilitation. Five people who have had a stroke (LE experts) and 18 workshop attendees (including the LE experts' caregivers) were organized into five groups. Following the Discover, Define, and Develop phases of the Double Diamond model, each group used Journey Maps and Data Collection Notebooks to co-create a solution for an objective specified by an LE expert. Journey Maps were thematically analyzed, and group audio transcripts underwent structured extraction using a large language model. LE input shifted discussions from discipline-based assumptions toward priorities grounded in lived experience, including fear, confidence, sensory discomfort, fatigue, aesthetics, and independence. Narrow objectives, such as opening a blister pack, produced task-specific solutions, whereas broader objectives generated device concepts, including sensory feedback for cycling and a stimulation-equipped cooking splint. Satisfaction was high among attendees and LE experts. All workshop tools and protocols are shared to support adoption. The workshop offers a practical foundation for involving LE experts early in neurorehabilitation technology development. Future iterations should better capture psychological and social dimensions, allow more reflection, and include the full range of relevant stakeholders through sustained transdisciplinary engagement between researchers and the LE community.
- 2026-11-08, **From Sketch to Design: A Cross-scale Workflow for Procedural Generative Urban Design**, Jin Gao et.al., Paper: [@2611.11561](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11561)
> **Abstract:** Urban designers use sketches and symbols to express spatial intentions, while many generative urban design tools rely on numeric parameters that are difficult to relate to this visual process. We propose a cross-scale workflow and a web-based prototype that translates symbolic sketches into controllable procedural urban generation. A vocabulary of shapes, informed by examples of urban design diagrams and sketching exercises with graduate urban design students, encodes attributes such as network structure, density, and program. We describe the methodology and illustrate prototype interactions, then discuss downstream integration with architectural-scale generative tools for further refinement and analysis. The work connects designers' symbolic input with an explicit procedural generation process.
- 2026-11-03, **OmniScribble: Mixed-Initiative Authoring of Animated Sketches through Inspectable and Repairable AI Decisions**, Hien Trinh et.al., Paper: [@2611.04284](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04284)
> **Abstract:** AI-assisted creative tools reduce the effort of authoring content, but delegating creative decisions to AI can make it difficult for authors to understand, correct, or redirect work. Rather than generating animation end-to-end, OmniScribble presents AI proposals at verification checkpoints, where authors can accept, repair, or regenerate them. - 2026-11-01, **Capable but Not Scalable: Requirements and Interaction Costs of Building Multi-View CAD Datasets for Design Research**, Danyal Sarfraz et.al., Paper: [@2611.02108](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02108)
> **Abstract:** Transforming CAD models into consistent multi-view image datasets is increasingly important for AI-driven product design research, yet existing workflows can impose substantial burdens on researchers without programming expertise. We examine this challenge from a human-computer interaction (HCI) perspective by analyzing ten reported rendering practices and deriving fourteen requirements spanning dataset quality, visual consistency, and user interaction. We then assess eleven development- and design-oriented tools against an illustrative task involving STEP models, interpreting potential interaction costs through the cognitive dimensions framework. Our analysis reveals a gap between technical capability and practical scalability: programming-based frameworks offer flexible automation but require coding expertise, while graphical design tools provide accessible functionality yet retain repetitive per-product operations. ## 3D Understanding: Reconstruction & Generation

- 2026-11-12, **BrickFlow: Connectivity-Guided Brick Reconstruction**, Peter Kulits et.al., Paper: [@2611.17321](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17321)
> **Abstract:** Given a set of LEGO parts and an image of an assembled object, our task is to infer the pose of each part to assemble the object. In contrast with traditional 3D reconstruction, doing so requires satisfying discrete physical constraints, which provide structure but render the problem combinatorial and highly sensitive to small pose errors. To learn a prior over how parts fit together, we train a set-conditioned SE(3) flow model on procedurally generated assemblies. We then finetune this model to inject image conditioning, using 1% as much data. However, while the learned flow captures global structure, it does not enforce connectivity. To address this, we introduce an analytic connectivity-field guidance to pull predictions toward connected assemblies at test time. Through this, we demonstrate improved build quality with model size, pretraining, and guidance. We make our models and data available for research purposes at https://kulits.github.io/BrickFlow/
- 2026-11-12, **Spatial Foundry: A Multimodal Dataset for Spatial Intelligence**, Ethan Lin et.al., Paper: [@2611.17318](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17318)
> **Abstract:** Spatial intelligence requires connecting visual observations to the geometry, objects, and persistent state of an environment. Images alone leave these relationships implicit, while disconnected annotations make it difficult to study appearance, camera motion, and scene changes under a common reference. We introduce Spatial Foundry, a collection of 5,280 furnished residential 3D scene configurations with editable assets and aligned visual, geometric, and temporal data. Its modalities include floor plans, perspective and panoramic RGB, metric depth, point clouds, meshes, instance-level 3D bounding boxes, and camera trajectories paired with video. Shared scene coordinates link observations across views and time; editable source scenes enable additional camera paths and controlled object changes. We describe the data representation and construction workflow, and discuss applications in floor-plan-conditioned 3D generation, geometric reconstruction, instance-level perception, camera-conditioned video generation, shared world modeling, and scene editing. Controlled camera and scene variations support spatial perception and world-model research. The public project page provides videos and an interactive colored point-cloud preview.

> **Abstract:** We present Kosmo-GFM, a scene reconstruction system that integrates a geometry foundation model (GFM), a pose optimization engine (PoseEngine), and reconstruction-derived supervision from KosmoWorld. GFM predicts camera parameters, depth, cross-view correspondences, and dynamic 3D points from images and videos. > **Abstract:** Dynamic reconstruction from casual video must preserve valid subject motion while suppressing transient occluders, shadows, and volumetric effects. > **Abstract:** Reconstructing dynamic scenes and objects from monocular egocentric video is crucial for embodied AI, yet existing 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS) approaches struggle with everyday interactions involving topological changes, such as cutting, assembly, opening, or closing. Since 4DGS methods rely on deforming a fixed representation, events that split, merge, or reveal surfaces cause severe visual artifacts. To address this challenge, we introduce TopoEgo, the first benchmark dedicated to topological changes in egocentric video (covering fission, fusion, open, and close), alongside Topo-4DGS, a topology-aware reconstruction method. > **Abstract:** 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a mainstream representation for real-time novel view synthesis and 3D reconstruction. > **Abstract:** Predictive video encoders provide dense visual representations, but adapting them to 3D reconstruction requires accurate depth, camera poses, and cross-view geometry. - 2026-11-10, **FastWild-GS: Minutes-Scale In-the-Wild Gaussian Splatting via Compact Per-Image Appearance Adaptation**, Donghun Ryu et.al., Paper: [@2611.13808](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13808)

We introduce Motion Regime Expert Routing for Gaussian Splatting (MoRE-GS), a causal, render-aware framework for forecasting dynamic 3D Gaussian scenes. MoRE-GS freezes a Gaussian representation reconstructed exclusively from the observed temporal prefix and forecasts its control-keypoint motion using four complementary experts: persistence, a coefficient-space Transformer, a graph convolutional network, and a structured Transformer with rigid and non-rigid motion constraints. A compact routing table assigns expert mixtures across soft motion groups and forecast-horizon bins, while a persistence gate suppresses unreliable extrapolation. Across D-NeRF and HyperNeRF scenes, MoRE-GS achieves improved performance against other methods, and these results demonstrate that renderer-calibrated composition of complementary motion predictors improves causal dynamic Gaussian forecasting across heterogeneous motion regimes.   Project page: https://advanced-vision-and-learning-lab.github.io/MoRE-GS-website/
- 2026-10-30, **Coarse3D: Controllable and Faithful Sparse Structure Generation for Image-to-3D**, Gaige Wang et.al., Paper: [@2610.41231](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41231)
> **Abstract:** Coarse-to-fine 3D-native generation relies heavily on the first-stage sparse structure, which determines the global orientation and overall geometry for subsequent refinement. However, existing coarse generators suffer from two key limitations: their orientation is difficult to control due to ambiguous semantic canonicalization, and their structures are often inconsistent with input images due to weak 2D-3D grounding. We propose Coarse3D, a sparse-structure generation model that produces orientation-controllable and structurally faithful voxel geometry from arbitrary unposed images. Coarse3D introduces three designs: a first-frame anchored canonical space that aligns generation with the first input view while preserving object-centric normalization; a dual-tower DiT that couples generation with auxiliary feed-forward reconstruction to learn spatially grounded features; and spatial-prior guided attention that regularizes cross-attention with pixel-to-voxel correspondence. ## Embodied AI & Robot Learning

- 2026-11-12, **EgoX: Egocentric Cross-Embodiment Manipulation with Embodiment Dreaming**, Yaru Niu et.al., Paper: [@2611.16728](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16728)
> **Abstract:** Generalist manipulation policies can benefit from the complementary experience of humans, real robots, and simulated agents. However, differences in morphology, sensing, and control interfaces make it difficult to learn effectively from their combined demonstrations. We introduce EgoX, an egocentric cross-embodiment learning framework that organizes heterogeneous demonstrations through semantically corresponding observation and action modalities. EgoX pretrains a Modularized Cross-Embodiment Transformer with Embodiment Dreaming (MXT+) on this mixture and fine-tunes it on target-embodiment demonstrations. Within each embodiment, MXT+ assigns a dedicated tokenizer to each observation modality and a dedicated expert to each action modality, with a Transformer trunk shared across embodiments. A training-only embodiment-dreaming objective predicts latent representations of future visual and proprioceptive states, encouraging the shared trunk to model each embodiment's dynamics. Across 13 task-embodiment pairs spanning five tasks and six robot embodiments, including five physical robots and one simulated humanoid, cross-embodiment pretraining raises MXT+'s average normalized task progress from 0.63 to 0.80 over training from scratch. The gains are particularly pronounced under out-of-distribution conditions, where average task progress increases from 0.53 to 0.77. Ablations demonstrate the benefits of broader pretraining mixtures, modular interfaces, and embodiment dreaming, with dreaming producing more similar latent geometry across embodiments. Project page: egox-dream.github.io.
- 2026-11-11, **MiniPose: Miniature-Based Real Data Collection for 6D Pose Estimation of Large Industrial Pipe Segments**, Hyunjun Kim et.al., Paper: [@2611.15066](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15066)
> **Abstract:** 6-DOF pose estimation of large and heavy industrial objects, such as large piping systems in shipyards and structural components in petrochemical plants, remains relatively underexplored in robotic perception. We propose MiniPose, a practical pipeline for miniature-based real RGB-D data collection that addresses both the data-acquisition burden and the sim-to-real gap. We fabricate a scaled-down replica and capture RGB-D data of it in diverse poses using a real RGB-D camera. - 2026-11-11, **FOE-Dreamer: Deployment-Efficient Learning of Cyber Defense Policies in Operational Networks**, Yinuo Du et.al., Paper: [@2611.14880](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14880)
> **Abstract:** Autonomous cyber defense is widely studied with deep reinforcement learning, but most work trains policies inside simulators because learning requires many trial-and-error interactions. Simulator-based training carries a fidelity gap: trained policies face authentication delays, partial action failures, service dependencies, and user traffic patterns that the simulator did not represent, and degrade on transfer. Training directly in the network being defended removes the gap but makes every interaction slow and costly, so sample efficiency becomes the binding constraint. Separating the components reduces the variance in policy gradients during planning in imagination. Against two scripted attacker profiles with user activity emulated by the GHOSTS framework on an 8-host OpenStack testbed, FOE-Dreamer trains within a 3-day budget on a single GPU and achieves about 2$\times$ better episode loss than Rainbow and IQN baselines under matched compute. - 2026-11-10, **Koopman Model-Based Reinforcement Learning**, Wenjian Hao et.al., Paper: [@2611.14186](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14186)
> **Abstract:** We propose a Koopman model-based reinforcement learning (K-MBRL) algorithm for controlling nonlinear robotic systems with unknown dynamics. To this end, we develop a stochastic Koopman operator-based learning method to approximate the unknown nonlinear dynamics in a linear representation, while a critic function estimates the remaining future cost. This formulation offers two key advantages. First, the linear Koopman dynamics simplify the model-dependent policy gradient computation to a constant matrix product, enabling computationally efficient policy improvement. Second, one-step model propagation mitigates the accumulation of prediction errors associated with long-horizon rollouts while retaining long-term cost information through the critic. The learned policy enables real-time control through feedforward evaluation without online optimization. The proposed K-MBRL is further validated on a physical quadruped robot performing a challenging sequence of maneuvers: rising to an upright posture supported by two rounded rear feet, maintaining balance during bipedal walking, and safely returning to the ground, as shown in Fig.1.

> **Abstract:** World Action Models (WAMs) have recently emerged as a promising paradigm for robotic manipulation, endowing policies with strong capabilities for modeling temporal and physical dynamics. Existing WAMs, encompassing both video-first action prediction and joint video-action modeling, directly transfer global physical representations from world models to action generation. > **Abstract:** End-to-end navigation in cluttered environments relies on compact visual representations to support collision avoidance and goal-directed motion. Existing methods typically learn these representations jointly with the policy or through observation reconstruction, which does not explicitly specify what information the representation should preserve. > **Abstract:** In the absence of extrinsic rewards or task supervision, human behavior is guided by intrinsic motivation---neural signals that drive curiosity, creativity, play, and other essential facets of intelligence. > **Abstract:** Tactile sensing is an indispensable modality for contact-rich dexterous manipulation, a capability that hints at the ultimate frontiers of embodied intelligence. > **Abstract:** Early successes on agentic robotics point to a potential paradigm shift toward using coding agents to control robots. > **Abstract:** World models are widely considered to be a potential key component of model-based reinforcement learning and robot planning, but typically require task-specific training on substantial interaction data. > **Abstract:** Policies for dexterous manipulation depend on design choices made before training: which sensors enter the observation, where they are placed, and how their readings are arranged for the encoder.
