A unified benchmark interface and verified human-teleoperated demonstrations enable testing different policy learning algorithms, with standardized simulation configurations, scene randomization, trajectory replay, and task-level evaluation. Through extensive baseline experiments, we identify substantial challenges in precise manipulation, generalization to scene variation, and skill composition. DexArena establishes a challenging and reproducible benchmark for developing robot learning methods that move toward generalizable long-horizon dexterous behavior.
- 2026-11-02, **Energy-Based Policy Orchestration**, Weirui Ye et.al., Paper: [@2611.02546](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02546)
> **Abstract:** Hierarchical control scales robot behavior by separating task-level planning from low-level control. In dynamic, high-dimensional tasks, however, a fixed planner-to-policy interface can fail when contacts, balance, or disturbances evolve faster than high-level policy updates. Execution therefore requires choosing not only which low-level policy to run, but also which policy-specific goal should be given to it. We formulate this problem as policy orchestration: runtime selection of a policy-goal pair that is likely to remain feasible while tracking the planner target. We develop EPO, an energy-based policy orchestrator for modular goal-conditioned policy families. For each policy, EPO learns a composite energy over low-level goals, where lower energy corresponds to higher feasibility and more accurate target realization. At runtime, EPO refines each policy's goal and executes the lowest-energy policy-goal pair. Because energies are policy-specific, new policies can be incorporated by learning their own energy models, without retraining the existing policies or a centralized scheduler. In simulation, EPO improves the empirical precision-robustness trade-off by refining policy-specific goals before selectively switching among policies based on energy scores. We further demonstrate sim-to-real deployment of the same frozen policies and energy models on real humanoid loco-manipulation tasks. Project website: https://yewr.github.io/epo
- 2026-11-01, **GoalDex: Learning Image-Goal-Conditioned Policies for Dexterous In-Hand Manipulation**, Arjun Bhardwaj et.al., Paper: [@2611.02171](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02171)
> **Abstract:** Dexterous in-hand manipulation is commonly formulated around explicit object pose estimation, yet reliable pose tracking is challenging under severe hand-object occlusions and often requires object-specific supervision. This dependence creates a bottleneck between perception and control that can limit robustness and scalability. We present an end-to-end visuomotor framework for dexterous in-hand reorientation that operates on RGB observations and proprioception without intermediate pose estimation. Our approach jointly learns visual representations and control policies through behavior distillation from privileged state teachers and auxiliary reconstruction objectives. Furthermore, we introduce image-goal-conditioned reorientation, allowing desired object configurations to be specified directly through images rather than canonical pose representations. We evaluate our approach across a diverse set of object geometries and instances in simulation, where it consistently outperforms pose-based methods and policies built on pretrained visual encoders. Finally, we demonstrate direct sim-to-real transfer of a single image-goal-conditioned policy across five real-world objects, showing that accurate dexterous reorientation can be achieved without explicit pose estimation. For videos and code release, please refer to the project website: https://rffr.leggedrobotics.com/works/goaldex/
- 2026-11-01, **Reinforcement Learning in High-Energy Physics: A Survey from Accelerator Control to Theory Exploration**, Luca Anzalone et.al., Paper: [@2611.02049](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02049)
> **Abstract:** Reinforcement learning (RL) is a machine learning paradigm in which an agent learns a decision-making policy by interacting with an environment, guided by a scalar reward rather than explicit supervision. Supervised and unsupervised learning have become indispensable tools in high-energy physics (HEP), but they learn from fixed datasets, whereas many HEP tasks are closed-loop decisions: the data an algorithm observes depend on its choices, the feedback is sparse or delayed, and the objective is often measured on a real machine or in a stochastic simulation, not by a differentiable model. This survey reviews the application of RL to such tasks: accelerator control and feedback, data quality monitoring, jet grooming, clustering, and representation, charged particle tracking, beyond-the-Standard-Model (BSM) and string model building, detector design, trigger control, analysis, and formal quantum field theory computation. It provides a self-contained introduction to the underlying RL methods, from model-free and model-based to multi-agent, offline, inverse, and bandit approaches, reviews each domain against its traditional methods, and summarizes the head-to-head comparisons. The applications range from policies deployed on real accelerators and evaluated on recorded collision data to exploratory studies in formal theory. RL is most advantageous for repeated tasks with a fast simulator, decisions inside fast control loops, operation under faults and corrupted measurements, physics-based rewards in place of labels, and sequential discrete constructions with a reusable policy, while Bayesian optimization, genetic algorithms, and differentiable methods remain competitive elsewhere. We conclude with the open challenges that recur across domains, including reward design, sim-to-real transfer, safety, the lack of shared benchmarks, and the statistical validity of policy-driven decisions.
- 2026-11-01, **HuG: Learning Humanoid Whole-Body Affordance-Aware Grasping with Active Perception**, Xuanbin Peng et.al., Paper: [@2611.01659](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01659)
> **Abstract:** Acquiring objects in human environments is hard for humanoids: targets are partially occluded under egocentric viewpoints, and reaching them demands coordinated whole-body motion that keeps the target visible and reachable while the base stays stable. We present HuG, a framework for whole-body affordance-aware grasping with active perception that couples perception and action through a Perceive-Align-Grasp pipeline: belief-driven active perception resolves occlusion by treating viewpoint as an explicit decision variable; a point-cloud-conditioned affordance policy proposes whole-body grasps; and an affordance-aware alignment stage repositions the body to the most executable configuration. A key insight is that, on a floating base, kinematic validity is not enough: we curate training data with controller-in-the-loop dynamics simulation, rejecting 73% of kinematically valid grasps that would destabilize the deployment controller, so the policy learns kinodynamically feasible affordances. Trained entirely in simulation with zero real demonstrations, HuG attains 87.5% real-world success on a multi-level shelf task, generalizes to novel objects, and holds at least 73% success at every tested height. Beyond generalizing within its training distribution, the policy extrapolates spatially: at scale in simulation it succeeds at table heights well below any seen during training, indicating that kinodynamically filtered synthetic data grounds affordances in object geometry and whole-body feasibility rather than in memorized configurations.
- 2026-11-01, **$TF^2$-Steering: Action Refinement with Tactile Forecasts and Feedback via Velocity-Field Steering**, Yiming Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.01483](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01483)
> **Abstract:** Tactile sensing plays a crucial role in everyday human interaction. Consider inserting a plug into a tight socket, vision alone provides limited guidance during the final insertion stage, and we instead rely on both anticipated contact and realized tactile feedback to ajust the insertion motion. The same capability is equally important for robots, particularly in dexterous manipulation tasks. Yet many contemporary robot policies remain vision-dominant. This visual bias, compounded by limited tactile data and sparse contact events, can cause directly incorporated tactile signals to be ignored or even compromise the policy's pretrained capabilities, yielding degrading performance as observed in our experiments. We present $TF^2$-Steering, which refines actions via learned residual steering of the flow-matching action expert's velocity field, using a tactile mixture-of-experts architecture to integrate both tactile forecasts and real-time tactile feedback. Together with specific designed three stages training recipe, $TF^2$-Steering enables tactile signals to correct and, when necessary, redirect the flow-matching action-generation process, while building on the strong action-generation capacity of the vision-based foundation policy. Extensive experiments across six simulated and six real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of $TF^2$-Steering. It outperforms the strongest evaluated baselines by 10.7 percentage points in average simulation success rate and 9.1 percentage points in average real-world progress success rate. Project page: https://yiming-cc.github.io/tf2-steering/.
- 2026-10-30, **MolmoSpaces-CommonSense: Evaluating Commonsense in Robotic Manipulation Policies**, Arjun Guru et.al., Paper: [@2610.41377](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41377)
> **Abstract:** Real-world robotic manipulation requires reasoning about cluttered scenes, partially observable or hidden objects, and semantically appropriate actions, in addition to low-level execution within those scenes. However, existing simulation benchmarks primarily test the latter and remain dominated by comparatively clean environments with limited clutter and narrowly scoped task structure, leaving many forms of commonsense reasoning untested. We address this by introducing a taxonomy of reasoning processes for robotic manipulation grounded in developmental cognitive science. We operationalize this taxonomy through a new benchmark of robot tasks, MolmoSpaces-CommonSense, that requires reasoning about occlusion, containment, support relations, semantically correct grasp points, spatial affordances, and hidden objects. These evaluations require policies not only to recognize relevant scene structure but also to execute correct actions in them. We evaluate several zero-shot generalist manipulation policies. Across model types and architectures, we observe consistent failures in commonsense reasoning: success rates in cluttered scenes drop by over 50% as distractors increase, policies act on incorrect objects in cluttered settings 30% of the time, and affordance-sensitive grasping remains unreliable even with highly specific language prompts, with correct object part selection happening only about 50% of the time. Further, VLM-based success estimation struggles to reliably evaluate rollouts in these settings, suggesting that the challenge extends beyond control to the broader perception-reasoning-action stack. Our results suggest that existing benchmarks may substantially overestimate zero-shot real-world readiness. Code and policy leaderboard link at https://github.com/allenai/molmospaces.
- 2026-10-30, **HandMimicX: Learning Human-Like Dexterous Manipulation via an Object-Centric Policy from Egocentric Videos**, Zihan Gao et.al., Paper: [@2610.41336](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41336)
> **Abstract:** Learning dexterous manipulation from human videos remains an open challenge, particularly in the context of transferring a single demonstration into a spatially generalizable robotic skill and maintaining physically plausible human-like interactions. Unlike prior works that emphasize hand-motion imitation, we introduce an object-centric framework for acquiring dexterous skills from egocentric human RGB-D videos. Within our framework, we formulate the reinforcement learning (RL) objective in terms of object motion to decouple the underlying skill from the specific hand trajectory and impose constraints on hand-object interaction states to promote more anatomically natural hand configurations. ## Neural Rendering & Physics Simulation

- 2026-11-12, **Learning the Missing Derivative: Neural Multiple-Scattering Operators for Inverse Volume Rendering**, Sipeng Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.16650](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16650)
> **Abstract:** Reconstructing optically thick media from photographs requires modeling multiple scattering and its response to changes in extinction. Frozen-source tomography discards this derivative; differentiable path tracing estimates it through expensive, noisy sampling. We instead predict the multiple-scattering field with a neural operator and differentiate through it during inverse rendering, while computing transmittance and single scattering directly. A further challenge arises during optimization: an operator that predicts the field accurately on held-out media can still produce poor derivatives at the density states visited by the optimizer. We address this by fine-tuning on states generated during inversions driven by the operator, using Monte Carlo scattering-field supervision. On held-out optically thick clouds, our method improves relighting over frozen-source tomography at equal time and outperforms differentiable path tracing given substantially more time. Training on inversion trajectories also improves derivative alignment at visited states and reduces optimization drift.
- 2026-11-10, **A Lightweight Differentiable Method for Real-Time Skin Rendering and Digitization**, Mario Marquez-Balduque et.al., Paper: [@2611.14451](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14451)
> **Abstract:** Inverse rendering for skin typically relies on offline path tracing to estimate physical properties, which fail to map directly to the approximate shaders utilized in interactive applications, such as game engines. We bridge this gap by directly estimating subsurface scattering parameters tailored for real-time rendering pipelines. We introduce a differentiable Screen-Space Subsurface Scattering (SSSS) framework that enables the joint optimization of spatially varying base textures and scattering distances. To account for local variations in skin thickness, we estimate these parameters across distinct facial regions, generating a blended profile map ready for production runtime engines. Our pipeline utilizes a low-cost, single-light, single-view capture setup designed for accessible and practical appearance acquisition. Optimization is driven by a masked loss function targeting regions where subsurface scattering effects are dominant. We validate our approach on a real-world dataset of $45$ individuals across the Monk Skin Tone Scale, ensuring demographic generalization. Furthermore, we complement this evaluation with synthetic data, a multi-light setup validating our single-light assumption, and a tilted-view configuration confirming angular consistency. Results demonstrate that our method effectively recovers scattering parameters from lightweight inputs, yielding realistic skin appearance directly compatible with standard real-time shaders.
- 2026-11-10, **DepthGS: Depth-Guided Gaussian Splatting with Adaptive Regularization for High-Quality Surface Reconstruction**, Xiao Ren et.al., Paper: [@2611.13628](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13628)
> **Abstract:** Recently, 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as an efficient representation for novel view synthesis, offering fast differentiable rendering and high visual fidelity, and has increasingly been extended to surface reconstruction. However, the relationship between depth information and reconstruction error remains insufficiently explored, limiting the principled design of geometric optimization strategies. To fill the above gap, we propose DepthGS, a depth-guided Gaussian splatting framework. We derive theoretical relations between depth discrepancies and reconstruction error under single-view and multi-view settings, and rigorously establish corresponding upper bounds on reconstruction error. The above analysis informs the design of adaptive regularization strategies in both single-view and multi-view optimization. More specifically, for single-view optimization, we propose a depth-difference-guided adaptive normal regularization strategy that modulates the normal constraint strength using a geometric reliability weight computed from local depth differences. For multi-view optimization, we introduce a cross-view depth consistency regularization strategy that promotes consistency between neighboring-view depth maps and the corresponding depth predictions obtained by transforming the current view's depth predictions into those neighboring views. Extensive experiments on DTU, Mip-NeRF360, and Tanks and Temples demonstrate that DepthGS achieves competitive performance in both surface reconstruction and novel view synthesis.
- 2026-11-09, **Differentiable Inverse Rendering Recovers Intrinsic Properties from Intravascular Optical Coherence Tomography**, Ajay Manicka et.al., Paper: [@2611.12686](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12686)
> **Abstract:** Medical images encode intrinsic tissue properties that indicate disease progression, yet these properties remain entangled with the system-dependent physics of image formation. Differentiable inverse rendering asks whether they are recoverable by inverting the imaging system's forward physics rather than through post-hoc correction. We address this in optical coherence tomography (OCT), focusing on the optical attenuation coefficient, a per-pixel marker of pathology and cellular composition. Conventional recovery relies on closed-form inversions limited to simplified light-tissue models, or learned methods coupled to specific imaging configurations. We instead recover per-pixel attenuation from a single OCT image by gradient descent through a differentiable physics-based forward model. We verified the method against analytical solutions, validated it on five in vitro phantoms, and applied it without modification to 29{,}269 clinical intravascular OCT images from 128 patients. The inversion needs no training data or segmentations and is portable across platforms and retrospective archives. Replacing the forward model or recovering another optical parameter leaves the optimization procedure unchanged. We demonstrate per-pixel recovery is tractable because gradient information scales to this high-dimensional problem, where gradient-free methods become intractable. Our work supports quantitative tissue characterization from OCT images and provides a framework for extension across imaging modalities and organ systems.
- 2026-11-08, **SoftPAT3D: Physics-Augmented Text-to-3D Scene Generation with Deformables**, Lei Shu et.al., Paper: [@2611.11087](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11087)
> **Abstract:** We present SoftPAT3D, a physics-augmented framework for text-to-3D scene generation in which deformable objects, such as cloth, stuffed toys and ropes, are generated as physically valid, simulation-ready bodies and placed in intersection-free contact with the furniture that supports them, with one another and with themselves. Existing physics-augmented pipelines simulate rigid bodies only and cannot accommodate a generated deformable object, which text-to-3D generation returns as a geometrically invalid mesh, sometimes self-intersecting, that has no rest shape, no material model and no admissible initial state. These are the inputs a deformable simulation requires, and we construct each from information already in the pipeline. The rest shape comes from how the object is manufactured rather than from the generated physically inconsistent deformed configuration, and a material library assigns each deformable object its initial elastic parameters, which can then be optimized. The occluded support is regenerated and converted into a watertight collision mesh, each object is released from an optimizable placement above the supporting part that the prompt specifies, and the complete scene is driven to static equilibrium under one unified contact model, with completion verified by geometric checks and a vision-language model. When a scene fails the prompt, a grid search over placements corrects it, and the adjoint of a differentiable simulation of the same contact energy reaches the targets the grid does not, places several bodies jointly and recovers and optimizes material parameters. Multi-body scenes equilibrate in a single simulation, and the pipeline also runs from the text prompt alone. On all sixty-five prompts, SoftPAT3D produces no undesired interpenetration of any kind, whereas every other system does, and its scenes directly support scene editing and robotic manipulation.
- 2026-11-07, **ZARA: Zero-shot Animatable and Relightable 3D Avatar reconstruction from a single image**, Yixiao Chen et.al., Paper: [@2611.10562](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10562)
> **Abstract:** Recent large human reconstruction models create remarkable animatable 3D avatars from a single image, but their appearance remains entangled with the illumination and cannot be relighted. In contrast, existing 3D relightable-avatar reconstruction methods typically require person-specific optimization. We present ZARA, a zero-shot feed-forward framework that reconstructs an animatable 3D Gaussian avatar together with its intrinsic attributes from a single image. ZARA couples a pretrained large-scale human reconstruction model with a Material Transformer to inherit geometry priors while learning material-aware Gaussian representations. To preserve localized details, we introduce Gaussian-aligned material feature fusion strategy, which injects multi-scale material features onto individual Gaussians and combines them with geometry tokens. As most human-avatar datasets lack ground-truth material maps, we use pseudo-material labels as conditioning inputs and supervision targets during training. We further introduce MatFace regularization to preserve facial structure and color in albedo prediction under unreliable facial pseudo-material supervision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ZARA reconstructs animatable and relightable humans within tens of seconds without person-specific optimization or post-processing, outperforming existing baselines in both relighting quality and materials generalization ability. As the ability of recovering the physically-based-rendering attributes of the human, ZARA also enables direct material editing of the avatar's appearance.
- 2026-11-05, **Dynamic GPU Voxel Grids and Voxel-sequential Processing in NanoVDB**, Eftychios Sifakis et.al., Paper: [@2611.08665](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08665)
> **Abstract:** We introduce two fundamental advances to the NanoVDB data structure: dynamic GPU-native topological updates, and a voxel-sequential access paradigm that dramatically accelerates point-wise and stencil-based operators on sparse grids. NanoVDB's linearized memory layout has delivered strong GPU performance, and the Index-Grid concept introduced in the fVDB framework [Williams et al 2024] provided topology/data separation and optimal compactness of storage in sparse machine learning workloads. However, all these prior iterations of NanoVDB assume a static topology that has precluded use of this data structure in simulation and dynamic geometric processes. We eliminate this limitation by introducing a suite of dynamic topology operators -- morphological dilation, grid merging, pruning, and cross-resolution coarsening and refinement. These operators use bit-parallel algorithms to reconstruct grids containing hundreds of millions of voxels in just a few milliseconds.   We also introduce a voxel-sequential access paradigm and data structure for GPU kernels operating on sparse data. We stream active voxels as a flat linear sequence, minimizing or eliminating the need for tree traversal in the process. This achieves compute density and regularity that allow even complex stencil-based operators to approach peak memory bandwidth, with minimal storage overhead. Together, these contributions remove the read-only constraint from NanoVDB, establishing it as a high-performance primary representation for dynamic GPU simulation and geometry manipulation. We evaluate the efficacy of our newly introduced operators in benchmarks sourced from applications such as 3D reconstruction, fluid simulation, and large-scale mesh voxelization.
- 2026-11-05, **4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering**, Adnan Armouti et.al., Paper: [@2611.08342](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
> **Abstract:** Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.
- 2026-11-05, **PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State**, Jihai Zhang et.al., Paper: [@2611.07867](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07867)
> **Abstract:** Building a playable world requires more than generating plausible video: it demands responsive control, persistent spatial structure, and coherent interactions with dynamic entities. Using generated frames as the sole recurrent world representation ties these requirements to view-dependent appearance synthesis, making it difficult to maintain long-term spatial and temporal consistency in generation and interaction. - 2026-11-05, **Integrating Learned Priors through Prior-Space Reparameterization for Physically Based Inverse Rendering**, Hun Hwangbo et.al., Paper: [@2611.07573](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07573)
> **Abstract:** Physically based inverse rendering decomposes appearance into materials and illumination, an ambiguous problem for which learned material predictions provide useful prior information. - 2026-11-02, **IMUdrape: Generating Garment-Mounted Virtual IMU Data from Text through Physics-Based Cloth Simulation**, Zikang Leng et.al., Paper: [@2611.02393](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02393)
> **Abstract:** Inertial sensors are moving into clothing, from sensorized sportswear to garments instrumented with hundreds of IMUs. > **Abstract:** Laser doppler vibrometer (LDV) scans can be used to detect defects and cracks in manufactured parts before they are deployed. - 2026-10-30, **SketchForge: Training-Free Scene-to-Vector Sketching via Decoupled Structure and Texture Optimization**, Quang-Thinh Nguyen et.al., Paper: [@2611.00253](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00253)
> **Abstract:** Converting a raster image into a resolution-independent, stroke-editable vector sketch while faithfully preserving both structural contours and tonal texture remains challenging: raster-space rendering pipelines are difficult to edit at the stroke level, while generation-from-scratch vectorization methods struggle to remain faithful to an arbitrary input image. Project page: https://khangcntn123.github.io/SketchForge

## Narrative & Long-form Audio

- 2026-11-09, **SCLAP: Temporal structural alignment improves localization in long-form audio**, Yacouba Kaloga et.al., Paper: [@2611.12124](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12124)
> **Abstract:** Audio-text models such as Contrastive Language-Audio Pretraining (CLAP) typically pool frame and token sequences uniformly into global embeddings. This discards temporal structure and impairs both retrieval and localization, especially for short events in long recordings. We introduce SCLAP, a sequence-level contrastive framework that aligns audio frames and text tokens using monotone one-dimensional optimal transport. Its learned marginals and transport plan provide fine-grained temporal supervision from clip-level captions alone. On short-form audio (i.e., Clotho and TACOS datasets with 15-30s recordings), SCLAP remains competitive for retrieval while improving zero-shot localization over CLAP and Text-aware Attention Pooling. On long-form audio (i.e., CASTELLA dataset with up to 300s recordings), SCLAP improves retrieval performance and its localization advantage grows as the event-to-context ratio decreases. At inference, SCLAP requires no optimal transport computation since retrieval can use pooled embeddings and localization can use inexpensive frame-text cosine scores.
- 2026-11-05, **FoleyForge: Free-form, Controllable, Rectified-flow Editing of Sound Effects**, Giovana Morais et.al., Paper: [@2611.08695](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08695)
> **Abstract:** We present FoleyForge, a generative model for instruction-based editing of sound effects. Given an input clip and a free-form instruction describing the desired change, it applies the edit while preserving the input's identity. A single model handles semantic, stylistic, and DSP-like changes. We introduce a data pipeline that guides an audio language model to author both DSP-based acoustic edits and similarity-preserving semantic edits, thus addressing the lack of publicly available audio editing data. Such data is then used to fine-tune a rectified-flow audio generation model, which we extend to expose attribute controls for loudness change, edit strength, and an input-derived contour envelope, each tunable via classifier-free guidance, yielding a more expressive editing model. FoleyForge achieves state-of-the-art instruction adherence at competitive audio quality and source faithfulness.
- 2026-10-30, **MFlowAudio: Efficient Text-to-Audio Synthesis via Mamba-based Stateful Flow Matching**, Hao Dai et.al., Paper: [@2610.41852](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41852)
> **Abstract:** Recent advancements in audio generation have been largely driven by Transformer-based diffusion models. However, these models suffer from quadratic complexity of self-attention, severely bottlenecking the long-form audio synthesis. To overcome this limitation, we propose MFlowAudio, a novel latent audio generation framework that synergizes the continuous-time dynamics of Flow Matching with a custom-designed TCMamba backbone. TCMamba uses an innovative dual-scan mechanism: a TimeMamba module to capture long-range causal dependencies with linear complexity, and a ChannelMamba module to model cross-channel correlations such as harmonic structures. Exploiting this structural foundation, we formulate the Stateful Flow Matching (SFM) paradigm. This framework inherently enables chunk-wise training and streaming generation, maintaining an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ caching complexity without incurring extra computational overhead. To enable fine-grained controllable synthesis, we devise a novel guidance mechanism that neutralizes vector field collisions precipitated by on-the-fly semantic transitions of prompts. Comprehensive empirical evaluations confirm that MFlowAudio yields generative fidelity on par with state-of-the-art baselines while establishing significant computational efficiency, achieving above $14.5$% acceleration in generation and facilitating perceptually seamless streaming synthesis with a constant latency of $1.8$ seconds. Demo: https://huggingface.co/spaces/mflowaudio/MFlowAudio
- 2026-10-30, **Hierarchical Dual-Graph Retrieval for Long-form Audio Reasoning**, Eunsang Lee et.al., Paper: [@2610.41390](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41390)
> **Abstract:** Large audio language models (LALMs) struggle with long-form audio reasoning, as processing entire recordings often limits to capture query-relevant content distributed across distant segments while incurring substantial computational cost. We introduce AudioHelix, a hierarchical DualGraph framework tailored for LALMs' retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems. Unlike existing text-only or non-graph RAG approaches, AudioHelix connects related textual and acoustic evidence across temporally distant segments for multi-hop reasoning. Extensive experiments on TraceAV-Bench, MMAU-Pro, and AudioMarathon demonstrate that AudioHelix consistently outperforms full-audio processing and existing RAG methods. The project site is https://github.com/ewha-mmai/audiohelix.
